Angular · RxJS · JavaScript · TypeScript · HTML · CSS

Reactive Handbook

Simple interview notes on Angular, RxJS, JavaScript, TypeScript, HTML, and CSS — in easy words, with an example for each one.

Part N · Scenario-based questions
Performance scenarios
A page with a 5,000-row table is slow. What do you do? The app gets slower the longer a user keeps it open. How do you find the cause? The first load of the app takes 12 seconds. How do you reduce it? The same API is called 5 times when a page loads. How do you fix it? The search box sometimes shows the results of an older search. Why, and what is the fix? Users click Save twice and get duplicate records. How do you stop it? The browser freezes when a component renders, and the console shows NG0100 or an endless loop. What do you check? Users upload Excel files with 50,000 rows and the browser hangs. What do you do?
Data, API & security scenarios
A user is filling a long form and the token expires. What should happen? Different roles must see different menus, pages, and buttons. How do you design it? The backend changes an API response and your screen breaks. How do you protect against it? A security audit found an XSS issue. How do you fix it and prevent more? The business wants live notifications and offline support. What would you use? How would you cache API data in an Angular app? The same build must run in dev, QA, and production with different API URLs. How? After a deployment, some users still see the old version. Why?
Architecture & design scenarios
How do you decide between a service with signals, NgRx, and local component state? Your project still uses NgModules. How would you move to standalone components? You must build a form with 200 fields that changes by customer type. How? How do you design a reusable table or dropdown that many teams will use? Would you use micro frontends for this project? The marketing pages in your Angular app must be found on Google. What do you do?
JavaScript, TypeScript & CSS scenarios
You inherit a large JavaScript project. How do you move it to TypeScript? Production shows "Cannot read properties of undefined". How do you handle it? The layout works on desktop, but breaks on phones. How do you debug and fix it? A dropdown is hidden behind another element, even with a huge z-index. Why? A callback logs "undefined" for this.name, though the method worked a minute ago. What happened?
Team, process & delivery scenarios
A critical bug is reported in production on a Friday evening. What do you do? How do you give code review feedback, and how do you take it? You are asked for an estimate, and later you will miss the deadline. What do you do? You must change old, untested code that nobody understands. How do you do it safely? The requirements are unclear and the product owner is not available. What do you do?
Part O · HR & behavioral questions
About you
Tell me about yourself. What are your strengths? What is your weakness? Why are you leaving your current company? Why do you want to join us? Where do you see yourself in five years?
Teamwork & situations
What is the STAR method? Tell me about a conflict with a teammate, and how you handled it. Tell me about a mistake or a failure. How do you handle pressure and tight deadlines? How do you work with a difficult team member or manager? How do you keep your skills up to date? Have you led or mentored anyone?
Salary & joining
What are your salary expectations? What is your notice period? Can you join earlier? Do you have any questions for us? Why should we hire you?
00

Quick reference

Every entry in this handbook, in one line each. Jump to any row's full write-up from the outline on the left.

Name Category In one line
Observable Foundation Sends data over time. Nothing happens until something subscribes to it.
Observer Foundation The next / error / complete functions you pass into subscribe().
Subject Subject Sends data only to current subscribers. Does not store old values.
BehaviorSubject Subject Subject that stores the latest value and gives it to new subscribers right away.
ReplaySubject Subject Subject that stores old values and replays them to new subscribers.
AsyncSubject Subject Sends only the last value, and only after complete() is called.
switchMap Transformation Cancels the old call and switches to the newest one.
mergeMap Transformation Runs all the calls at the same time, in parallel.
concatMap Transformation Runs the calls one by one, in order.
exhaustMap Transformation Ignores new calls while one is already running.
debounceTime Filtering Waits for a pause before sending the latest value.
throttleTime Filtering Sends the first value immediately, then blocks values for a fixed time.
distinctUntilChanged Filtering Skips a value if it's the same as the one right before it.
takeUntil Filtering Unsubscribes automatically when another Observable emits.
takeUntilDestroyed Filtering Auto-unsubscribes when the component is destroyed (Angular 16+, no manual Subject).
combineLatest Combination Sends the latest values from every source whenever any one of them changes.
forkJoin Combination Waits for every source to finish, then sends all the results together.
zip Combination Pairs values from each source Observable by their position.
withLatestFrom Combination Takes the latest value from another Observable whenever the main one emits.
race Combination Takes the value from whichever Observable emits first; ignores the rest.
tap Utility Runs a side effect — like logging or a loader — without changing the data.
catchError Utility Catches a failed Observable and returns fallback data instead.
finalize Utility Runs cleanup code when the stream finishes, errors, or is unsubscribed.
shareReplay Utility Caches a response so every subscriber reuses it instead of calling the API again.
Why Signals Angular feature Better performance and fine-grained reactivity — only the affected UI parts update.
effect() Angular feature Runs a side effect automatically whenever a signal it reads changes.
computed() Angular feature Creates a derived signal that recalculates when its dependencies change.
Signal Inputs Angular feature Receive a component input as a signal with input(), instead of @Input() (Angular 17+).
Signals vs. RxJS Angular feature Signals handle local UI state; RxJS still handles async operations and events.
toSignal() Angular feature Converts an Observable into a signal, kept in sync automatically.
toObservable() Angular feature Converts a signal into an Observable, so RxJS operators can be used on it.
linkedSignal() Angular feature A writable signal whose default value is linked to — and follows — another signal.
resource() Angular feature Manages loading, success, and error state for async data using signals.
httpResource Angular feature Reactive HTTP data fetching built on signals, without manual subscribe().
model() Angular feature A writable signal for two-way binding between a parent and a child component.
Dependency Injection Angular feature Angular creates and provides services to components, instead of components creating them.
@Injectable() Angular feature Marks a class as available for Angular's Dependency Injection system.
Service Angular feature A reusable class holding business logic, API calls, or shared functionality.
Providers Angular feature Tell Angular how to create and supply a service.
Singleton Service Angular feature One shared instance of a service, used across the whole application.
providedIn vs. providers Angular feature providedIn gives one app-wide, tree-shakable instance; providers gives a new instance per scope.
Multi Provider Token Angular feature Lets several providers share one token instead of replacing each other.
Data Binding Angular feature Connects component data (TypeScript) with the HTML template.
Interpolation Angular feature Displays a dynamic value inside HTML, using double curly braces.
Property Binding Angular feature Sends data from TypeScript to an HTML element's property, using [property].
Event Binding Angular feature Sends data from HTML to TypeScript when a user acts, using (event).
Two-way Binding Angular feature Syncs data both ways between component and view, using [(ngModel)].
What is Angular? Angular feature A Google frontend framework for building single page web applications.
Building blocks Angular feature Components, templates, modules, services/DI, and routing.
Component Angular feature The building block that controls one part of the UI: class, template, and styles.
Component-based architecture Angular feature The app is split into small, reusable, independent components.
Single Page Application Angular feature One HTML page loads once; content updates without a full page refresh.
Angular architecture Angular feature How components, services, DI, modules, and routing fit together.
Angular CLI Angular feature A command-line tool to create, run, build, and generate parts of an app.
NgModule Angular feature Groups components, directives, pipes, and services, and tells Angular how they fit together.
Standalone Components Angular feature Components that don't need an NgModule, manage their own dependencies (Angular 15+/17+).
How Angular works internally Angular feature Bootstraps from main.ts, builds the component tree, binds data, runs change detection.
Constructor Angular feature Initializes the class and injects dependencies; runs before lifecycle hooks.
ngOnInit() Angular feature Lifecycle hook that runs after the component is created — used for API calls and setup.
Sharing data (unrelated) Angular feature A shared service with an Observable or BehaviorSubject that components subscribe to.
Passing data to components Angular feature @Input()/@Output(), a shared service, router params, or global state (NgRx/Signals).
Smart vs. Dumb components Angular feature Smart handles logic and API calls; dumb only displays data and emits events.
State management Angular feature How data is stored, updated, and shared — services, Signals, or NgRx/NGXS/Akita.
@ViewChild() Angular feature Accesses a child component, DOM element, or directive from the parent.
@ViewChildren() Angular feature Accesses multiple child components or elements, returned as a QueryList.
@ContentChild() Angular feature Accesses content a parent projected into the component via ng-content.
TemplateRef Angular feature A reference to an <ng-template>, not rendered until a view is created from it.
ViewContainerRef Angular feature A DOM location where Angular can add or remove views dynamically.
Dynamic Component Loading Angular feature Creates and renders a component at runtime, e.g. for modals or dashboards.
ng-container Angular feature Groups elements with a structural directive, without adding an extra DOM element.
Change Detection Angular feature How Angular checks component data and updates the DOM when it changes.
Default vs. OnPush Angular feature Default checks the whole tree every cycle; OnPush checks only on input reference change.
Why is OnPush faster? Angular feature It skips unnecessary checks by relying on input reference changes, not value mutation.
Zone.js Angular feature Tracks async operations so Angular knows when to run change detection.
NgZone Angular feature The Angular service built on Zone.js that triggers change detection for async work.
runOutsideAngular() Angular feature Runs code without triggering Angular's change detection.
ngZone.run() Angular feature Brings code back inside Angular's zone so change detection runs again.
Zone-less Angular Angular feature Runs without Zone.js — change detection is triggered manually instead of automatically.
detectChanges vs. markForCheck Angular feature detectChanges updates the view immediately; markForCheck queues it for the next cycle.
Selector Angular feature The custom HTML tag name Angular uses to render a component.
template vs. templateUrl Angular feature Inline HTML in the decorator, versus HTML loaded from a separate file.
View Encapsulation Angular feature Controls whether a component's styles stay scoped to it or become global.
@defer Angular feature Lazy-loads part of a template only when a trigger (viewport, interaction, timer) fires.
forwardRef Angular feature Tells Angular to resolve a class reference later, once it exists.
JIT vs. AOT Angular feature JIT compiles templates in the browser; AOT compiles them ahead of time, at build.
Ivy Renderer Angular feature Angular's modern compiler and rendering engine — faster, smaller, easier to debug.
Tree Shaking Angular feature Removes unused code from the build to shrink the bundle.
Avoiding memory leaks Angular feature Clean up subscriptions and timers with unsubscribe(), takeUntil(), or the async pipe.
Checking performance Angular feature Angular DevTools, the Chrome Performance/Network tabs, Lighthouse, and bundle analyzers.
Optimizing performance Angular feature OnPush, trackBy, lazy loading, the async pipe, caching, and unsubscribing properly.
trackBy Angular feature Tells *ngFor how to identify items, so only changed ones re-render.
Lifecycle overview Angular feature The order Angular calls its hooks in, from constructor to ngOnDestroy.
ngOnChanges() Angular feature Runs whenever an @Input() property's value changes.
ngDoCheck() Angular feature Runs on every change detection cycle — for custom, manual change checks.
ngAfterContentInit() Angular feature Runs once after content is projected in via ng-content.
ngAfterContentChecked() Angular feature Runs after every check of the projected content.
ngAfterViewInit() Angular feature Runs once the component's view and child views are initialized.
ngAfterViewChecked() Angular feature Runs after every check of the component's view and child views.
ngOnDestroy() Angular feature Runs just before the component is destroyed — the place to clean up.
Angular Routing Angular feature Maps URL paths to components, for navigation inside a single page app.
Lazy / Eager / Preloading Angular feature Load a module on demand, at startup, or in the background after startup.
Route Guards Angular feature CanActivate, CanActivateChild, CanMatch, CanDeactivate, and Resolve.
AuthGuard Angular feature Checks whether a user is allowed to access a route.
Resolver Angular feature Preloads a route's data before the component opens.
CanMatch Angular feature Decides if a route should match at all, before Angular loads it.
CanDeactivate Angular feature Confirms before letting a user leave a page, e.g. with unsaved changes.
CanActivateChild Angular feature Protects every child route of a parent route at once.
Query Parameters Angular feature Values passed in the URL, e.g. for pagination or filters.
Role-based access Angular feature A guard checks the stored user role before allowing a route.
Wildcard Route Angular feature path: '**' catches any unmatched URL, usually for a 404 page.
Default Route Angular feature Redirects an empty path ('') to a starting page, like a dashboard.
Data in routes Angular feature A route's data property stores static values like a title or role.
Reactive vs. Template-driven Angular feature Logic in TypeScript with FormGroup, vs. logic written in the HTML template.
FormGroup Angular feature Groups a fixed set of form controls together as one object.
FormArray Angular feature Manages a dynamic list of form controls as an array.
Dynamic Forms Angular feature Builds form fields at runtime from a config object or API data.
Pipe Angular feature Transforms data for display in a template, without changing the original value.
Pure vs. Impure Pipe Angular feature Pure runs only when input changes; impure runs on every change detection cycle.
pipe() before subscribe() Angular feature Applies RxJS operators to process data before it reaches subscribe().
Directives Angular feature Change a DOM element's behavior or appearance — component, structural, or attribute.
Structural Directives Angular feature Add or remove elements from the DOM, like *ngIf and *ngFor.
Attribute Directives Angular feature Change an existing element's style or behavior, like ngClass and ngStyle.
Custom Directive Angular feature A hand-written directive, e.g. highlighting an element on hover.
HttpClient Angular feature Angular's service for sending HTTP requests and getting an Observable back.
Calling an API Angular feature Inject HttpClient in a service, call get()/post()/etc., then subscribe in the component.
Interceptor Angular feature Sits in the middle of every HTTP call, to add tokens, log, cache, or handle errors globally.
Handling API errors Angular feature catchError in services, plus interceptors for global cases like auth failures.
404 / 500 error pages Angular feature A wildcard route for 404s; an interceptor to catch and route 500s.
SSR Angular feature Renders the page on the server first, for a faster, SEO-friendly first load.
Hydration Angular feature Makes a server-rendered page interactive, by attaching Angular in the browser.
InjectionToken Angular feature A unique DI key for injecting non-class values, like a config string.
index.html Angular feature The entry HTML file the browser loads; holds the root selector.
package.json Angular feature Project metadata, dependencies, devDependencies, and npm scripts.
package-lock.json Angular feature Locks the exact installed versions of every package, for consistent builds.
angular.json Angular feature The Angular CLI config for building, serving, and testing the app.
tsconfig.json Angular feature Tells the TypeScript compiler how to compile the project.
main.ts Angular feature The entry point that bootstraps the Angular application.
app.module.ts Angular feature The root module grouping components, directives, pipes, and services.
dependencies vs. devDependencies Angular feature Packages the app needs to run, vs. packages needed only to develop it.
Custom Validators Angular feature A function that checks a control's value and returns an error object, or null.
Async Validators Angular feature A validator that checks a value against an API, returning an Observable/Promise.
Cross-field validation Angular feature Compares multiple fields together, like matching password fields.
FormBuilder Angular feature A helper service that reduces the boilerplate of building reactive forms.
ControlValueAccessor Angular feature Lets a custom component work like a native input inside Angular forms.
@Host / @Self / @SkipSelf / @Optional Angular feature Fine-tune where Angular looks for a dependency in the injector tree.
@HostBinding() Angular feature Binds a class, style, or attribute of the host element to a class property.
@HostListener() Angular feature Runs a method when an event fires on the host element.
Renderer2 vs. ElementRef Angular feature The safe, cross-platform way to change the DOM, instead of touching it directly.
exportAs Angular feature Names a directive so a template reference variable can access it directly.
XSS & DomSanitizer Angular feature Angular auto-sanitizes bound values; DomSanitizer opts a trusted value out.
environment.ts files Angular feature Per-environment config, like the API URL, swapped in automatically at build time.
Angular CLI commands Angular feature ng generate, ng serve, ng build, ng test, ng add, ng update.
TestBed Angular feature Builds a mini Angular module for tests, so components/services work as in the app.
Testing a Component Angular feature Create it with TestBed, then assert on the instance and the rendered template.
Testing a Service Angular feature Inject it from TestBed and call its methods directly.
HttpTestingController Angular feature Tests HttpClient code against fake requests, without a real API.
Mocking with spies Angular feature Replaces a real dependency's methods with fakes, to isolate what's under test.
@if, @for & @switch Angular feature Built-in control flow that replaces *ngIf, *ngFor, and *ngSwitch, with no imports needed.
@let declarations Angular feature Store a value in a template variable once, and reuse it.
inject() function Angular feature Get a dependency without a constructor, in classes, functions, and guards.
output(), viewChild() & contentChild() Angular feature Signal-based replacements for @Output and the query decorators.
effect() cleanup & untracked() Angular feature Run cleanup before an effect re-runs, and read a signal without tracking it.
DestroyRef Angular feature Register cleanup code from anywhere, without implementing ngOnDestroy.
Content projection with ng-content select Angular feature Project different content into different slots of a component.
ng-template & ngTemplateOutlet Angular feature Define a template once, and render it with data wherever you need it.
ngClass, ngStyle & class/style bindings Angular feature Three ways to apply classes and styles dynamically.
Directive composition (hostDirectives) Angular feature Attach directives to a component from inside its decorator.
Writing a custom pipe Angular feature A class with a transform method; keep it pure.
ExpressionChangedAfterItHasBeenChecked Angular feature A binding changed during the same check that rendered it; change it earlier, or in the next tick.
detach, reattach & ApplicationRef.tick Angular feature Take a component out of change detection, and run it manually.
ngOnChanges & SimpleChanges Angular feature See the previous and current value of each changed @Input.
Common Angular errors Angular feature NullInjectorError, NG0100, NG0303, NG0201, and what each one means.
router-outlet, routerLink & child routes Angular feature Nested routes render into a router-outlet inside their parent component.
ActivatedRoute: snapshot vs. paramMap Angular feature snapshot reads the value once; paramMap emits again when the same component stays and the parameter changes.
router.navigate vs. navigateByUrl Angular feature navigate takes a commands array and is relative-aware; navigateByUrl takes a full URL string.
Router events & loading indicator Angular feature Listen to navigation start, end, error, and cancel.
Preloading strategies Angular feature PreloadAllModules loads every lazy route in the background; a custom strategy chooses which.
Router configuration options Angular feature Scroll restoration, hash location, title, input binding, and relative links.
Functional guards with inject() Angular feature A guard is just a function that returns true, false, or a UrlTree.
Typed reactive forms Angular feature FormControl, FormGroup, and FormArray carry types, so value and controls are checked.
setValue vs. patchValue Angular feature setValue needs every field; patchValue accepts a part of them.
Form states: touched, dirty & valid Angular feature pristine/dirty track changes; touched/untouched track focus; valid/invalid/pending track validation.
valueChanges & statusChanges Angular feature An Observable of every change in a control or whole form.
updateOn: blur & submit Angular feature Run validation on blur or submit instead of every keystroke.
ngModel vs. formControlName Angular feature Template-driven forms use ngModel; reactive forms bind controls you create in the class.
FormArray: add and remove rows Angular feature A dynamic list of controls or groups, with add and remove methods.
Functional interceptors Angular feature Write an interceptor as a function and register it with withInterceptors.
JWT auth & refresh token flow Angular feature Attach the access token, and on a 401 refresh it once and retry the request.
HttpParams, headers & request options Angular feature Build query strings and headers; use observe and responseType for more than the body.
File upload with progress Angular feature Send FormData and listen to progress events.
retry, timeout & firstValueFrom Angular feature Retry failed calls with a delay, give up after a time limit, and convert to a Promise.
XSRF, CSP & other security Angular feature Cookie-to-header CSRF protection, Content Security Policy, and trusted types.
CORS & proxy.conf.json Angular feature A dev proxy forwards API calls so the browser sees one origin.
Source maps Angular feature Files that map minified production code back to your original TypeScript for debugging.
Angular build system: esbuild vs. webpack Angular feature The application builder uses esbuild and Vite for much faster builds than the older webpack builder.
Bundle budgets Angular feature angular.json can fail or warn the build when a bundle grows too large.
What ng build --configuration production does Angular feature AOT, minification, tree shaking, hashing, and no source maps.
Deploying an Angular app Angular feature Build, serve the static files, rewrite all routes to index.html, and set the base href.
Runtime config & multiple environments Angular feature Environment files are baked in at build time; load config.json at startup for true runtime config.
ESLint, Prettier & code quality Angular feature Lint for mistakes, format for style, and enforce both before commits.
Upgrading Angular versions Angular feature Use ng update one major version at a time, and read the update guide.
Schematics & ng generate Angular feature Code generators that create files and edit existing ones.
Angular Material & CDK Angular feature Material gives ready components; the CDK gives behaviours like overlay, drag-drop, and virtual scroll.
Virtual scrolling Angular feature Render only the rows visible on screen, so a list of 100,000 rows stays fast.
i18n & PWA Angular feature Translate the UI at build time, and add offline support with a service worker.
Core, shared & feature structure Angular feature Singletons in core, reusable pieces in shared, and one folder per feature.
Facade & service-layer pattern Angular feature A facade service hides several services and state behind one simple API.
NgRx: actions, reducers, effects & selectors Angular feature One-way flow: dispatch an action, a reducer makes the new state, effects handle side effects.
Micro frontends & monorepos Angular feature Split a big UI into independently deployed apps, or keep many apps and libraries in one repo.
Global ErrorHandler Angular feature Catch every unhandled error in one place, to log and show a message.
Title & Meta services Angular feature Change the page title and meta tags from code.
Angular Elements Angular feature Package a component as a standard Web Component for use in any page.
Testing async code: fakeAsync & waitForAsync Angular feature Control time in tests with tick(), and wait for promises to settle.
Karma, Jasmine, Jest & coverage Angular feature Jasmine is the assertion library, Karma the browser runner; Jest runs without a browser.
Debugging an Angular app Angular feature Source maps, breakpoints, Angular DevTools, and the ng global.
Angular version highlights Angular feature What arrived in each recent version, as interviewers often ask.
of, from & EMPTY RxJS of emits the values you give; from converts an array, Promise, or iterable; EMPTY completes at once.
interval, timer & fromEvent RxJS Streams from time and DOM events.
defer & lazy creation RxJS Delay creating the Observable until someone subscribes.
merge & concat RxJS merge combines streams as values arrive; concat plays them one after another.
map, filter & pluck RxJS map changes each value; filter keeps the ones that pass.
take, first, last & skip RxJS Take only a few values, then complete.
startWith, scan & reduce RxJS startWith adds an initial value; scan is a running total; reduce emits only at the end.
distinct, distinctUntilKeyChanged & filter patterns RxJS Remove duplicates in a stream, by value or by a key.
delay, debounce, auditTime & sampleTime RxJS Four ways to slow a fast stream down.
bufferCount, toArray, groupBy & partition RxJS Collect values into arrays, or split a stream by key.
combineLatestWith, mergeWith, forkJoin object & zip differences RxJS Pipeable versions of combination operators, and how they differ.
throwError, retry & catchError placement RxJS Where you put catchError decides whether the whole stream dies.
share vs. shareReplay RxJS share shares one subscription; shareReplay also replays the last values to late subscribers.
subscribe forms & unsubscribing RxJS Three ways to subscribe, and every way to unsubscribe.
Nested subscribe problem RxJS Never subscribe inside subscribe; use a higher-order mapping operator.
Subject vs. Observable & asObservable() RxJS Expose an Observable from a service so outside code cannot call next().
Observable vs. Promise vs. async/await RxJS Lazy, cancellable, multi-value streams versus one-time eager values.
Testing RxJS with marbles RxJS ASCII diagrams describe a stream over time, so tests can check timing.
What is JavaScript? JavaScript A single-threaded, dynamically typed language that runs in the browser and on Node.js.
ES6+ features JavaScript let/const, arrow functions, classes, modules, destructuring, spread, promises, and later additions.
var vs. let vs. const JavaScript var is function-scoped; let and const are block-scoped, and const cannot be re-assigned.
Data types JavaScript 7 primitives (string, number, bigint, boolean, undefined, null, symbol) plus object.
Primitive vs. reference types JavaScript Primitives are copied by value; objects are copied by reference.
typeof & checking types JavaScript typeof works for primitives; use Array.isArray, instanceof, and === null for the rest.
== vs. === JavaScript == converts types before comparing; === compares value and type with no conversion.
Type coercion JavaScript JS converts types automatically (implicit) or when you ask it to (explicit).
Truthy & falsy values JavaScript Only 8 values are falsy; everything else, including [] and {}, is truthy.
null vs. undefined JavaScript undefined means no value was given; null is an empty value set on purpose.
NaN & number quirks JavaScript NaN is a number that is not equal to itself; 0.1 + 0.2 is not exactly 0.3.
Template literals JavaScript Backtick strings with ${} interpolation and multi-line support.
'use strict' JavaScript Strict mode turns silent mistakes into errors; modules and classes are strict by default.
Scope & scope chain JavaScript Where a variable can be used: global, function, block, or module scope.
Hoisting JavaScript Declarations are registered before code runs; only var and function declarations are usable early.
Temporal Dead Zone JavaScript The time between entering a scope and the let/const line, when the variable cannot be used.
Closures JavaScript A function that remembers the variables of the scope it was created in, even after that scope finished.
Closures in loops (var vs. let) JavaScript var shares one variable across iterations; let creates a new one each time.
IIFE JavaScript A function that runs immediately after it is defined, creating a private scope.
Currying JavaScript Turning f(a, b, c) into f(a)(b)(c), one argument at a time.
Execution context & call stack JavaScript Each function call gets its own context, pushed onto and popped off the call stack.
Declaration vs. expression vs. arrow JavaScript Declarations are hoisted; expressions and arrows are not, and arrows have no own this.
Arrow functions JavaScript No own this, no arguments object, cannot be used with new.
The this keyword JavaScript this depends on how a function is called, not where it is written.
call, apply & bind JavaScript call and apply run a function with a chosen this; bind returns a new function for later.
Higher-order functions JavaScript Functions that take a function as an argument or return one.
Callback functions JavaScript A function passed to another function, to be called later.
Pure functions & side effects JavaScript Same input gives the same output, with no changes to anything outside.
Default params, rest & arguments JavaScript Default values for missing arguments, and ...rest to collect extra arguments into an array.
Ways to create an object JavaScript Literal, constructor function, class, Object.create, and Object.assign.
Prototype & prototype chain JavaScript Objects inherit from other objects through a chain of prototype links.
Classes JavaScript Syntax sugar over prototypes, with constructor, extends, super, static, getters, and #private fields.
What does new do? JavaScript Creates an object, links its prototype, runs the constructor, and returns the object.
Shallow vs. deep copy JavaScript A shallow copy shares nested objects; a deep copy duplicates everything.
Object.freeze vs. seal JavaScript freeze blocks all changes; seal allows editing existing properties only. Both are shallow.
Destructuring JavaScript Unpack values from arrays and properties from objects into variables.
Spread vs. rest JavaScript The same ... syntax: spread expands values out, rest collects them in.
Optional chaining & nullish coalescing JavaScript ?. reads safely through null or undefined; ?? falls back only for null or undefined.
Object.keys, values & entries JavaScript Turn an object into arrays of keys, values, or [key, value] pairs, and back.
map, filter & reduce JavaScript map transforms each item, filter keeps matching items, reduce combines all items into one value.
forEach vs. map JavaScript forEach runs code for each item and returns undefined; map returns a new array.
find, some, every & includes JavaScript find returns the first match; some and every return booleans; includes checks for a value.
slice vs. splice JavaScript slice copies part of an array; splice changes the original by removing or inserting.
Mutating vs. non-mutating methods JavaScript push, splice and sort change the array; map, filter, slice and toSorted return a new one.
flat & flatMap JavaScript flat removes nesting levels; flatMap maps and flattens one level in one step.
Map & Set JavaScript Set stores unique values; Map stores key-value pairs with any type of key.
WeakMap & WeakSet JavaScript Collections with object keys held weakly, so entries are garbage collected with their keys.
for...in vs. for...of JavaScript for...in loops over keys; for...of loops over values of iterables.
Symbol JavaScript A unique primitive used as a hidden or collision-free property key.
Iterators & generators JavaScript Generators are functions that can pause with yield and resume later.
Synchronous vs. asynchronous JavaScript Sync code runs line by line and waits; async code starts a task and handles its result later.
Event loop JavaScript Runs all sync code, then all microtasks, then one macrotask, and repeats.
Microtasks vs. macrotasks JavaScript Promise callbacks (microtasks) always run before the next setTimeout (macrotask).
Callback hell JavaScript Deeply nested async callbacks that are hard to read and handle errors in.
Promise JavaScript An object for a future result: pending, then fulfilled or rejected, exactly once.
Promise chaining JavaScript Each then returns a new promise, so steps run in order and one catch handles any failure.
Promise.all, allSettled, race & any JavaScript Run several promises together and choose how to combine their results.
async / await JavaScript async functions return promises; await pauses the function, not the thread, until a promise settles.
Error handling in async code JavaScript Use try/catch with await or .catch() with then; a try block cannot catch errors from later callbacks.
fetch & AbortController JavaScript fetch returns a promise that only rejects on network failure; AbortController cancels a request.
Web Workers JavaScript Run heavy JavaScript on a background thread so the page stays responsive.
What is the DOM? JavaScript The tree of objects the browser builds from HTML, which JavaScript reads and changes.
querySelector vs. getElementById JavaScript querySelector takes any CSS selector; getElementsBy* return live collections.
Event bubbling & capturing JavaScript Events travel down to the target (capture), then back up through its parents (bubble).
Event delegation JavaScript One listener on a parent handles events for all its children, including ones added later.
preventDefault vs. stopPropagation JavaScript preventDefault stops the browser's default action; stopPropagation stops the event bubbling up.
localStorage vs. sessionStorage vs. cookies JavaScript Where to store data in the browser, how long it lasts, and whether it goes to the server.
Debounce & throttle (write it yourself) JavaScript Debounce runs after the calls stop; throttle runs at most once per interval.
CORS JavaScript The browser blocks reading responses from another origin unless that server allows it with headers.
ES modules vs. CommonJS JavaScript import/export is static and tree-shakable; require is the older Node.js system.
IntersectionObserver JavaScript Tells you when an element enters or leaves the viewport, without scroll listeners.
Error handling & custom errors JavaScript try/catch/finally, built-in error types, and your own Error subclasses.
Garbage collection JavaScript The engine frees memory that is no longer reachable, using mark-and-sweep.
Memory leaks in JavaScript JavaScript Memory that stays reachable by mistake: timers, listeners, detached DOM, globals, growing caches.
Memoization JavaScript Cache a function's result for each input so repeat calls are instant.
Polyfill vs. transpiler JavaScript A polyfill adds a missing feature at runtime; a transpiler rewrites new syntax at build time.
Common design patterns JavaScript Module, singleton, observer, factory, decorator, and strategy, with an event bus example.
Output: hoisting & shadowing JavaScript A local var is hoisted inside the function and hides the outer one.
Output: event loop order JavaScript Sync logs first, then promise and await continuations, then setTimeout.
Output: this in objects JavaScript Regular methods get the object; arrow functions and plain callbacks do not.
Output: tricky comparisons JavaScript Classic coercion puzzles: [] == ![], null >= 0, and 'baNaNa'.
Output: references & closures JavaScript Re-assigning a variable does not change other references; each closure keeps its own state.
Write polyfills: map, filter, reduce & bind JavaScript Rebuild the array methods and bind, to show you understand callbacks and this.
Flatten a nested array JavaScript Recursively spread nested arrays into one flat array.
Deep clone (write it yourself) JavaScript Recursively copy objects and arrays, handling Dates and circular references.
Infinite currying: sum(1)(2)(3)() JavaScript Keep returning a function until it is called with no argument.
Implement Promise.all JavaScript Resolve with all results in order, or reject as soon as one fails.
Common array & string problems JavaScript Duplicates, frequency count, group by, palindrome, anagram, second largest, missing number.
Common string methods JavaScript slice, split, includes, replaceAll, padStart, trim, and the difference between slice, substring, and substr.
Numbers, Math & parsing JavaScript parseInt vs. Number, toFixed, rounding, random ranges, and Number.isInteger.
Date object JavaScript Months start at 0, dates parse differently by format, and time zones cause most bugs.
Regular expressions JavaScript Test, match, and replace text with patterns.
JSON JavaScript stringify and parse, what is lost, and how to handle errors.
Object.defineProperty & getters/setters JavaScript Control whether a property is writable, enumerable, or computed.
Proxy & Reflect JavaScript Intercept reading, writing, and deleting properties of an object.
WeakRef & FinalizationRegistry JavaScript Hold an object without keeping it alive.
Recursion & stack overflow JavaScript A function that calls itself needs a base case; deep recursion overflows the stack.
Inheritance vs. composition & mixins JavaScript Prefer building objects from small behaviours over deep class hierarchies.
Custom events JavaScript Dispatch and listen to your own events on any element.
MutationObserver & ResizeObserver JavaScript Watch for DOM changes and element size changes without polling.
requestAnimationFrame & requestIdleCallback JavaScript Run code before the next paint, or when the browser is idle.
URL & URLSearchParams JavaScript Parse and build URLs safely instead of concatenating strings.
File API, Blob & FileReader JavaScript Read local files, and create files for download.
WebSocket vs. Server-Sent Events vs. polling JavaScript Three ways to get live data from the server.
History API & location JavaScript Change the URL without a page reload; this is how single page app routers work.
Cookies JavaScript Small key-value data sent with every request; control with flags.
Clipboard API JavaScript Copy text with a user click.
Time complexity (Big O) JavaScript How the cost grows with the input: O(1), O(log n), O(n), O(n log n), O(n²).
Binary search & sorting JavaScript Halve a sorted array each step; know how sort works.
Two-sum, frequency maps & common patterns JavaScript Use a Map for lookups instead of nested loops.
Output questions: more classics JavaScript Predict the result: array holes, sort, parseInt in map, and var in closures.
Output question: async ordering with await JavaScript Work out the order of logs with await, then, and setTimeout.
More coding questions JavaScript once, compose, pipe, sleep, retry, chunk, groupBy, and flatten objects.
What is TypeScript? TypeScript JavaScript plus static types, checked at compile time and removed from the output.
TypeScript vs. JavaScript TypeScript TS adds static types and compile-time errors, then compiles down to plain JS.
Basic types TypeScript string, number, boolean, arrays, tuples, literal types, void, null and undefined.
Type inference vs. annotation TypeScript TypeScript guesses types from values; annotate parameters and anything it cannot infer.
any vs. unknown vs. never TypeScript any turns checking off; unknown must be narrowed before use; never is a value that cannot exist.
Enums TypeScript Named constants that exist at runtime; a union of string literals is often simpler.
Optional & readonly properties TypeScript ? makes a property optional; readonly stops it from being changed after creation.
interface vs. type TypeScript Both describe object shapes; type also handles unions and tuples, interface can be merged.
Union & intersection types TypeScript A union is one of several types; an intersection combines several types into one.
Type narrowing & type guards TypeScript typeof, instanceof, in, and equality checks narrow a union to one type.
Custom type guards (is) TypeScript A function returning x is Type tells TypeScript what a check proves.
Discriminated unions TypeScript Union members share a literal field, so a switch on that field gives exact types.
Type assertion & non-null assertion TypeScript as and ! tell the compiler to trust you; they add no runtime check.
Function types & overloads TypeScript Type a function's parameters and return; overloads give one function several signatures.
Index signatures TypeScript Describe objects whose keys are not known in advance.
Declaration merging TypeScript Interfaces with the same name merge, which is how you extend library types.
Generics TypeScript Type parameters like T let one function or interface work with many types safely.
Generic constraints TypeScript extends limits which types a generic accepts; K extends keyof T allows only real keys.
keyof & typeof TypeScript keyof gives a union of an object's keys; typeof gets the type of a value.
Indexed access types TypeScript Read the type of a property with T['key'], or of array items with T[number].
Partial, Required & Readonly TypeScript Make every property optional, required, or read-only.
Pick & Omit TypeScript Pick keeps only the listed keys; Omit removes them.
Record TypeScript An object type with known key and value types, often keyed by a union.
Exclude, Extract & NonNullable TypeScript Filter the members of a union type.
ReturnType, Parameters & Awaited TypeScript Get a function's return type, its parameter types, or the value inside a Promise.
Mapped types TypeScript Build a new type by looping over the keys of another type.
Conditional types & infer TypeScript T extends U ? X : Y chooses a type; infer pulls a type out of another.
Template literal types TypeScript Build string types from other string types, like btn-primary-sm.
satisfies operator TypeScript Checks a value against a type but keeps the exact inferred type.
Exhaustive checks with never TypeScript Assign the leftover case to never so a new union member causes a compile error.
Access modifiers TypeScript public, protected, private, readonly, and JavaScript's runtime-private #fields.
Parameter properties TypeScript private x: Type in the constructor declares and assigns the field in one step.
Abstract class vs. interface TypeScript An abstract class can share code and exists at runtime; an interface is only a shape.
implements vs. extends TypeScript extends inherits code from one class; implements only promises to match interfaces.
Decorators TypeScript Functions written as @Name that add metadata or behaviour to classes and members.
.d.ts files & @types TypeScript Type-only files that describe JavaScript code to TypeScript.
tsconfig strict options TypeScript strict turns on noImplicitAny, strictNullChecks, and the other safety checks.
Structural typing & excess property checks TypeScript TypeScript compares the shape of types, not their names.
object vs. Object vs. {} TypeScript Three look-alike types that mean different things.
Type widening & as const TypeScript let widens to a general type; const and as const keep the literal.
Tuples, readonly arrays & labels TypeScript Fixed-length arrays with a type per position.
Assertion functions & this types TypeScript A function that throws unless a condition holds, narrowing after the call.
InstanceType, ConstructorParameters & other utilities TypeScript Utility types that work with classes and strings.
Recursive & branded types TypeScript Types that refer to themselves, and types that stop mixing similar values.
Covariance & contravariance TypeScript Return types accept narrower types; parameter types accept wider ones.
import type & module options TypeScript Import only types so they are erased, and settings that affect modules.
How TypeScript compiles TypeScript tsc type-checks and emits JavaScript; types are erased.
override, satisfies & modern keywords TypeScript Keywords added in recent TypeScript versions.
TypeScript with ESLint & Babel TypeScript Type-checking, linting, and compiling are three separate jobs.
Enum vs. const object vs. union TypeScript Three ways to model a fixed set of values, with their trade-offs.
Generic classes & typed API layers TypeScript A reusable class or service typed by the data it handles.
What is HTML & HTML5? HTML The markup language for page structure; HTML5 added semantic tags, media, new inputs, and APIs.
DOCTYPE & page structure HTML DOCTYPE turns on standards mode; head holds metadata, body holds visible content.
Important meta tags HTML charset, viewport, description, robots, Open Graph, and Content-Security-Policy.
Semantic HTML HTML Tags that describe meaning (header, nav, main, article) for accessibility and SEO.
Block vs. inline elements HTML Block elements start on a new line and take full width; inline elements sit inside text.
id vs. class HTML An id is unique on the page; a class can be reused on many elements.
data-* attributes HTML Store custom values on elements; read them with dataset or target them in CSS.
Attribute vs. DOM property HTML Attributes are the initial HTML values; properties are the live values on the DOM object.
Forms & input types HTML form, label, input types, select, radio groups, textarea, and button types.
Built-in form validation HTML required, pattern, min, max, minlength, and type=email, checked by the browser.
Labels HTML A label names its input, so clicking it focuses the field and screen readers announce it.
Accessibility (a11y) basics HTML Semantic tags, alt text, labels, keyboard support, focus, and enough color contrast.
ARIA roles & attributes HTML Adds meaning for assistive tech when no native HTML element fits.
tabindex & keyboard focus HTML tabindex 0 adds an element to the Tab order; -1 makes it focusable only from code.
What happens when you type a URL? HTML DNS, connection, request, response, then parse, layout, paint, and run JavaScript.
How the browser renders a page HTML HTML to DOM, CSS to CSSOM, render tree, layout, paint, composite.
script: async vs. defer HTML defer runs in order after parsing; async runs as soon as it downloads, in any order.
Responsive & lazy images HTML srcset and sizes pick the right image size; picture adds formats; loading=lazy delays offscreen images.
preload, prefetch & preconnect HTML Resource hints that tell the browser what to fetch or connect to early.
canvas vs. SVG HTML SVG is vector shapes in the DOM; canvas is pixels drawn with JavaScript.
iframe HTML Embeds another page; use sandbox and frame headers for safety.
Web Components & Shadow DOM HTML Native custom elements with encapsulated DOM and styles.
SEO basics in HTML HTML Titles, descriptions, headings, semantic tags, fast pages, and SSR for single page apps.
Native dialog & details HTML Built-in modal dialogs and collapsible sections, with no JavaScript library.
Links: target, rel & downloads HTML Open links safely, and download files.
Table structure & accessibility HTML thead, tbody, th scope, caption, and spanning cells.
Useful input attributes HTML autocomplete, inputmode, disabled vs. readonly, and datalist.
Drag and drop, contenteditable & template HTML Native drag and drop, editable regions, and inert markup.
Entities & whitespace HTML Escape special characters, and know how the browser treats spaces.
Third-party scripts: SRI & crossorigin HTML Make the browser check that a CDN file was not changed.
Popover API & inert HTML Built-in popovers, and a way to disable a whole part of the page.
Core Web Vitals HTML LCP, INP, and CLS: the three numbers Google uses for page experience.
What is CSS & ways to add it CSS CSS styles HTML; add it inline, in a style tag, or in an external file.
Box model CSS Every element is a box of content, padding, border, and margin.
box-sizing CSS border-box makes width include padding and border, so sizes are predictable.
margin vs. padding CSS Padding is space inside the border; margin is space outside it.
Margin collapsing CSS Touching vertical margins merge into the larger one instead of adding up.
display property CSS block, inline, inline-block, flex, grid, none, contents, and flow-root.
display:none vs. visibility:hidden vs. opacity:0 CSS Only display:none removes the space; only opacity:0 stays clickable.
overflow & text truncation CSS Control content that does not fit: scroll, hide, or cut with an ellipsis.
Block formatting context (BFC) CSS An isolated layout area that contains floats and stops margin collapse with its children.
CSS selectors CSS Type, class, id, attribute, descendant, child, sibling, and group selectors.
Specificity CSS Decides which rule wins: ids beat classes, classes beat elements.
Cascade & inheritance CSS Origin, importance, layer, specificity, and order decide the winner; text properties are inherited.
!important CSS Overrides normal specificity; useful for utilities, but usually a sign of a specificity problem.
Pseudo-classes vs. pseudo-elements CSS :hover styles a state; ::before styles a part or generated content.
:has(), :is() & :where() CSS :has() selects a parent by its children; :where() groups selectors with zero specificity.
CSS custom properties (variables) CSS --name values that cascade, inherit, and can change at runtime.
position property CSS static, relative, absolute, fixed, and sticky.
z-index & stacking context CSS z-index orders positioned elements, but only within the same stacking context.
float & clearfix CSS float wraps text around an element; flow-root contains floated children.
Flexbox CSS One-dimensional layout in a row or a column, with alignment and spacing.
flex-grow, flex-shrink & flex-basis CSS How items grow into extra space, shrink when space is short, and their starting size.
justify-content vs. align-items vs. align-content CSS justify-content works on the main axis; align-items on the cross axis; align-content spaces wrapped lines.
CSS Grid CSS Two-dimensional layout with rows and columns, fr units, and named areas.
Flexbox vs. Grid CSS Flexbox for one direction and content-driven sizing; Grid for two-dimensional layouts.
How to center a div CSS Flexbox, grid place-items, absolute with transform, margin auto, and text-align.
CSS units CSS px, %, em, rem, vw/vh, dvh, ch, and fr, and when to use each.
rem vs. em CSS rem always follows the root font size; em follows the current element and compounds when nested.
Media queries CSS Apply styles by screen width, color scheme, motion preference, hover support, or print.
Mobile-first vs. desktop-first CSS Mobile-first writes base styles for phones and adds min-width queries for larger screens.
Responsive design principles CSS Viewport tag, fluid layouts, flexible images, relative units, and breakpoints.
clamp(), min() & max() CSS Fluid sizes with a minimum and a maximum, without many media queries.
Container queries CSS Style a component by the size of its container instead of the screen.
object-fit & aspect-ratio CSS Fit images inside a box without stretching, and reserve space with a fixed ratio.
Transition vs. animation CSS Transitions animate a state change; keyframe animations run on their own with many steps.
transform CSS Move, scale, and rotate an element without affecting the layout of others.
Reflow vs. repaint CSS Layout changes cause expensive reflows; color changes repaint; transform and opacity only composite.
SCSS / Sass CSS Variables, nesting, mixins, partials, and extends, compiled to plain CSS.
BEM naming CSS Block__Element--Modifier class names that keep specificity flat and intent clear.
Tailwind vs. Bootstrap CSS Tailwind gives utility classes for a custom design; Bootstrap gives ready-made components.
:host & ::ng-deep in Angular CSS :host styles the component element; ::ng-deep reaches into child components and should be scoped.
Dark mode & theming CSS Define colors as variables, switch them with prefers-color-scheme or a data-theme attribute.
@layer, @supports & nesting CSS Control the cascade with layers, test for features, and nest rules.
:focus-within, logical properties & gap CSS Style a parent when a child is focused, and write direction-friendly CSS.
scroll-snap, sticky & scroll behavior CSS Native carousels and smooth scrolling without JavaScript.
@font-face & font loading CSS Load custom fonts without invisible or jumping text.
filter, backdrop-filter, clip-path & shadows CSS Visual effects with no images.
contain & content-visibility CSS Skip rendering work for off-screen content.
pointer-events, user-select, cursor & appearance CSS Small properties that control how an element reacts to the user.
Text wrapping & typography properties CSS word-break, overflow-wrap, white-space, line-height, and text-wrap.
vertical-align & inline gaps CSS Why there is a gap under images and between inline-blocks.
3D transforms & perspective CSS Flip cards and depth effects.
CSS reset, critical CSS & architecture CSS Start from a consistent base, and keep the first paint fast.
A page with a 5,000-row table is slow. What do you do? Scenario Measure first, then cut what is rendered: pagination or virtual scroll, OnPush, trackBy, and fewer bindings.
The app gets slower the longer a user keeps it open. How do you find the cause? Scenario Take heap snapshots to find what keeps growing, then fix subscriptions, timers, listeners, and detached DOM.
The first load of the app takes 12 seconds. How do you reduce it? Scenario Analyze the bundle, lazy load routes, remove heavy libraries, enable compression and caching, and use @defer.
The same API is called 5 times when a page loads. How do you fix it? Scenario Find who subscribes, then share one request with shareReplay, a service cache, or a signal-based store.
The search box sometimes shows the results of an older search. Why, and what is the fix? Scenario It is a race condition between responses; switchMap cancels the previous request.
Users click Save twice and get duplicate records. How do you stop it? Scenario Disable the button while saving, use exhaustMap, and make the server idempotent.
The browser freezes when a component renders, and the console shows NG0100 or an endless loop. What do you check? Scenario A binding is changing during change detection, or a getter or effect keeps changing its own input.
Users upload Excel files with 50,000 rows and the browser hangs. What do you do? Scenario Do not parse on the main thread: upload to the server, or parse in a Web Worker and send in chunks, with progress.
A user is filling a long form and the token expires. What should happen? Scenario Refresh the token silently in an interceptor, replay the request, and never lose the form data.
Different roles must see different menus, pages, and buttons. How do you design it? Scenario Permissions from the token or API, a guard per route, a directive for UI, and the server as the real check.
The backend changes an API response and your screen breaks. How do you protect against it? Scenario Map API data in one place, type it, use versioned endpoints, and add contract checks.
A security audit found an XSS issue. How do you fix it and prevent more? Scenario Stop rendering untrusted HTML, sanitize, and add a CSP and review rules.
The business wants live notifications and offline support. What would you use? Scenario WebSocket or SSE for live data, a service worker for offline, and a queue for offline writes.
How would you cache API data in an Angular app? Scenario Match the cache to how often data changes: in-memory with shareReplay, HTTP caching headers, an interceptor, or a store.
The same build must run in dev, QA, and production with different API URLs. How? Scenario Load a config.json at startup (APP_INITIALIZER), so the build is not rebuilt per environment.
After a deployment, some users still see the old version. Why? Scenario index.html or the service worker was cached; fix cache headers and prompt for refresh.
How do you decide between a service with signals, NgRx, and local component state? Scenario Start with the smallest tool; add NgRx only for large, shared, complex state.
Your project still uses NgModules. How would you move to standalone components? Scenario Use the CLI migration in steps, with tests passing after each step.
You must build a form with 200 fields that changes by customer type. How? Scenario A config-driven dynamic form, split into sections, with reusable field components and validators.
How do you design a reusable table or dropdown that many teams will use? Scenario A small, typed API with inputs and outputs, content projection, accessibility, and documentation.
Would you use micro frontends for this project? Scenario Only when separate teams must deploy independently; otherwise a modular monolith is simpler.
The marketing pages in your Angular app must be found on Google. What do you do? Scenario Use SSR or prerendering, set titles and meta tags per route, and keep content out of client-only code.
You inherit a large JavaScript project. How do you move it to TypeScript? Scenario Move gradually: allowJs, then convert file by file, then tighten strictness.
Production shows "Cannot read properties of undefined". How do you handle it? Scenario Reproduce with the logged data, find the missing guard, fix the cause, and add monitoring and a test.
The layout works on desktop, but breaks on phones. How do you debug and fix it? Scenario Check the viewport tag, find overflowing elements, and make layouts fluid with grid or flex and relative units.
A dropdown is hidden behind another element, even with a huge z-index. Why? Scenario Stacking contexts: the dropdown's ancestor has a lower stacking order; render it at the body level.
A callback logs "undefined" for this.name, though the method worked a minute ago. What happened? Scenario The method was passed as a callback and lost its this; use an arrow function or bind.
A critical bug is reported in production on a Friday evening. What do you do? Scenario Confirm and size the impact, mitigate first (rollback or flag), then fix properly and follow up.
How do you give code review feedback, and how do you take it? Scenario Be specific and kind, separate must-fix from preferences, and treat feedback as learning.
You are asked for an estimate, and later you will miss the deadline. What do you do? Scenario Break the work down when estimating, and raise risk early with options.
You must change old, untested code that nobody understands. How do you do it safely? Scenario Add characterization tests first, make small safe changes, and avoid a big rewrite.
The requirements are unclear and the product owner is not available. What do you do? Scenario List assumptions and questions, work on what is clear, and confirm early with a small demo.
Tell me about yourself. HR Present, past, future in about 90 seconds: your role now, key experience, and why this role.
What are your strengths? HR Pick two or three that match the job, and back each one with a real example.
What is your weakness? HR Name a real, fixable weakness, and show what you are doing about it.
Why are you leaving your current company? HR Talk about growth and what you are moving toward, and never criticize your employer.
Why do you want to join us? HR Show you researched the company: product, tech, and how your skills fit.
Where do you see yourself in five years? HR A realistic path of growing skills and responsibility, in a way that fits the role.
What is the STAR method? HR Situation, Task, Action, Result: a way to tell a short, clear story.
Tell me about a conflict with a teammate, and how you handled it. HR Stay calm and professional: listen, find the real issue, agree on a fix, and focus on the result.
Tell me about a mistake or a failure. HR Own it, explain the fix and what you changed so it does not happen again.
How do you handle pressure and tight deadlines? HR Prioritize, communicate early, break down the work, and protect quality on the critical parts.
How do you work with a difficult team member or manager? HR Understand their point of view, communicate directly and respectfully, and stay professional.
How do you keep your skills up to date? HR Name specific habits: official docs, side projects, courses, and applying new things at work.
Have you led or mentored anyone? HR Even without a title: reviews, onboarding, knowledge sharing, and taking ownership of a module.
What are your salary expectations? HR Give a researched range, link it to the role and market, and stay flexible on the total package.
What is your notice period? Can you join earlier? HR State it honestly, and say what you can negotiate in a professional way.
Do you have any questions for us? HR Always ask two or three thoughtful questions about the work, team, and growth.
Why should we hire you? HR Connect your skills and results to what the job needs, in a confident and short answer.
Foundation Subject Transformation Filtering Combination Utility Pattern Angular feature JavaScript TypeScript HTML CSS
Part A · Angular core fundamentals
Angular core fundamentals

The building blocks every Angular app is made of.

01

Angular fundamentals

Overview

What is Angular?

Angular is a frontend framework developed by Google, used to build single page web applications.

  • Uses components, modules, services, and dependency injection to build scalable applications.
  • Supports TypeScript, and provides built-in features like routing, forms, and HTTP API integration.
Overview

What are the main building blocks of Angular?

Angular is built on five main building blocks that work together to create an application:

  • Components — define the UI and logic.
  • Templates — define the HTML structure.
  • Modules — organize the parts of the app.
  • Services & Dependency Injection — the reusable logic / business layer.
  • Routing — navigation between views.
Building block

What is a Component in Angular?

A component is the building block of an Angular application. It controls the part of the UI shown on the screen.

  • A TypeScript class for logic.
  • An HTML template for the UI.
  • A CSS file for styling.
Architecture

Component-based architecture

Component-based architecture means the application is divided into small, reusable, and independent components. Each one has its own HTML template, TypeScript logic, and CSS styles.

  • Improves code reusability, maintainability, and scalability.
  • Makes the application easier to maintain and scale.
Concept

What is a Single Page Application?

A Single Page Application (SPA) loads only one HTML page once, and updates content dynamically without refreshing the full page.

  • The whole page does not reload — only the component or content changes dynamically.
Architecture

Angular architecture

Angular architecture means how an Angular application is structured internally — mainly using components, services, modules, routing, and data binding.

  • Components — control the UI. Contain HTML, CSS, and TypeScript.
  • Services — contain business logic and API calls, injected using Dependency Injection.
  • Modules (or standalone components in Angular 17+) — organize features.
  • Routing — used to navigate between pages.
  • Dependency Injection — Angular creates and provides service instances automatically.
  • Data Binding — Interpolation, Property Binding, Event Binding, and Two-way Binding. See Data binding.
Tooling

What is Angular CLI?

Angular CLI is a command-line tool used to create, run, build, and manage Angular applications using commands, instead of writing everything manually.

  • Automates development tasks like generating components, services, and modules, and building the project.
Organization

What is NgModule?

NgModule is a container that organizes an Angular application by grouping components, directives, pipes, and services in one place. It tells Angular how the different parts of the app work together.

  • Declares components, directives, and pipes.
  • Imports other modules.
  • Defines which component starts the app (bootstrap).
Angular 15+/17+

Standalone Components

Standalone Components are components that don't need an NgModule. They are self-contained, and can be used directly.

  • Makes the project simpler, and reduces extra code.
  • Makes lazy loading easier, and improves performance.

With standalone components, modules are no longer required in many cases, because a component can manage its own dependencies directly. Standalone APIs let us build directives and pipes the same way, without declaring them inside a module.

Startup

How does Angular work internally?

Angular starts from main.ts, and loads the root module and root component — this is called bootstrapping.

  • Angular builds the UI using a component tree.
  • It converts HTML templates into JavaScript code, and creates the DOM.
  • It binds data between the template and TypeScript, and uses change detection to update the UI automatically when data changes.

In modern Angular (17+), we usually use standalone components, so app.module.ts is often not required — instead of bootstrapModule(AppModule), we use bootstrapApplication(AppComponent).

02

Project & configuration files

Entry file

Role of index.html in Angular

index.html is the main entry HTML file of an Angular application — the first file the browser loads. It contains the root selector, like <app-root>, which is where Angular starts.

  • Holds the page title, favicon, meta tags, and SEO tags.
  • Loads external scripts and styles.
Configuration

What is package.json?

package.json is the main configuration file of an Angular project. It contains project information, dependencies, dev dependencies, and npm scripts used to run and manage the application.

  • Project information — name, version, and similar metadata.
  • Dependencies — libraries required to run the application.
  • Dev dependencies — libraries used only during development.
  • Scripts — commands we execute, like start and build.

It's what lets npm install install every required package for the project.

Configuration

What is package-lock.json?

package-lock.json is an automatically generated file that stores the exact versions of every installed package and its dependencies. It ensures every developer, and every environment, installs the same versions — avoiding compatibility issues.

Should we commit it to Git? Yes, always. It:

  • Ensures consistent builds.
  • Prevents "works on my machine" problems.
  • Makes deployments reproducible.
  • Keeps team environments synchronized.
Configuration

What is angular.json?

angular.json is the main configuration file of an Angular project. It controls how the application is built, served, tested, and configured — Angular CLI uses it while building and running the app.

  • Build configuration — how Angular builds the application.
  • Serve configuration — controls ng serve.
  • Global styles — SCSS, Bootstrap, global CSS files.
  • Global scripts.
  • Environment configurations.
  • Assets — images, icons, fonts, JSON files.
Configuration

What is tsconfig.json?

tsconfig.json is the TypeScript configuration file — the instruction file for the TypeScript compiler, telling it how to compile TypeScript code into JavaScript.

  • Settings include the target JavaScript version, module type, strict type checking, path aliases, and other compiler options.
  • In Angular projects, it helps maintain type safety and project structure.
Entry point

What is main.ts?

main.ts is the entry point of an Angular application — the first file that executes, responsible for bootstrapping (starting) the app. In modern Angular, it uses bootstrapApplication() to start the root component and configure application providers.

  • Starting Angular, and loading the root component.
  • Registering HTTP services and routes.
  • Configuring providers, and handling startup errors.

Without main.ts, no Angular application starts.

Root module

What is app.module.ts?

app.module.ts is the root module of a traditional Angular application. It groups together the components, directives, pipes, services, and other modules the application needs.

  • Declarations — registers components, directives, and pipes.
  • Imports — imports other Angular modules.
  • Providers — registers services.
  • Bootstrap — defines the first component Angular loads.

In newer Angular versions, standalone components can be used instead of AppModule.

package.json

dependencies vs. devDependencies

dependencies — packages required to run the application. Without these, the app can't run.

Example: in an ERP application, dependencies like Angular, RxJS, and Angular Router are needed to display pages, call APIs, and navigate between screens.

devDependencies — packages needed only during development, to write, build, and test the app. Users of the built application don't need these.

Example: dev dependencies like Angular CLI, TypeScript, ESLint, and Karma help write code, compile TypeScript, and run tests — but aren't needed once the app is built.
03

Data binding

Template feature

What is Data Binding?

Data binding connects component data (TypeScript) with the HTML template (view) in Angular. It is used to show data and keep the UI in sync with the component, and the component in sync with the UI.

  • There are 4 types of data binding: Interpolation, Property Binding, Event Binding, and Two-way Binding.
Component → HTML

Interpolation

Interpolation is used to display dynamic values inside HTML, using {{ }}.

<p>{{ name }}</p>
Component → HTML

Property Binding

Property Binding is used to bind an HTML element's property dynamically. It sends data from TypeScript to HTML, using [property]="value".

<img [src]="imageUrl">
HTML → Component

Event Binding

Event Binding is used to respond to user actions like click, input, or submit. It sends data from HTML to TypeScript, using (event)="method()".

<button (click)="save()">Save</button>
Both directions

Two-way Data Binding

Two-way data binding keeps data synchronized between the UI and the component, in both directions. If the value changes in TypeScript, the UI updates automatically. If the user changes the value in the UI, the component's value updates too. We use [(ngModel)] for this.

  • Data flows both ways between HTML and TypeScript.
  • Value changes in TypeScript → UI updates.
  • User changes value in UI → TypeScript updates.
<input [(ngModel)]="name">
04

Component communication

TypeScript

Constructor in Angular

The constructor is a TypeScript feature used to initialize the class and inject dependencies, like services. It runs when the component is created, before Angular's lifecycle hooks.

constructor is a special method of a class that is automatically called when an object of that class is created. It is mainly used to initialize the object's data.

Lifecycle hook

ngOnInit() in Angular

ngOnInit() is a lifecycle hook that runs after Angular creates and initializes the component. It is mainly used for API calls and initialization logic.

Communication

How do you share data between unrelated components?

The best way is a shared service with an RxJS Observable or BehaviorSubject. The service stores the data, and multiple components subscribe to it.

Communication

How to pass data to components

There are a few common ways to pass data between components, depending on how they're related.

  • Parent → Child (@Input()): define @Input() in the child, and bind the data from the parent using property binding. Angular updates the child automatically when the parent's data changes — internally handled through the ngOnChanges lifecycle hook.
  • Child → Parent (@Output() + EventEmitter): create an @Output() with an EventEmitter, emit a value using .emit(), and the parent listens using event binding.
  • Shared Service + RxJS: a service with a Subject or BehaviorSubject — one sibling sends data, the other subscribes. Works for real-time communication, even if components are far apart.
  • Router parameters: pass data during navigation.
  • Global State (NgRx / Signals): centralized state management, used in larger apps like ERPs and dashboards.
  • Angular Signals (modern): signals inside a service — cleaner than RxJS, and better performance.
Pattern

Smart Components & Dumb Components

Smart components (container / parent components) handle logic, API calls, and state management — they control the application's data and business logic.

Dumb components (presentational / child components) only display data and emit events to the parent. They have no API calls or business logic, which makes them reusable and easy to test — they receive data through inputs, and send events through outputs.

Architecture

State Management

State management is how we store, update, and share data across the application. Examples of state: user data, API responses, form data, or UI state like a loading flag or a modal being open.

  • In small Angular apps, a service with BehaviorSubject or Signals is usually enough.
  • In large apps, libraries like NgRx, NGXS, or Akita manage state in a predictable way.
05

View & content queries

Query

@ViewChild() in Angular

@ViewChild() is used to access a child component, DOM element, or directive inside the parent component. It becomes available in ngAfterViewInit().

  • Call a child component's methods, or access its properties.
  • Access DOM elements, like an input or a button.
Use cases: focus an input field, trigger a child form's validation, expand or collapse a child component, scroll to an element, read a child's data.
Query

@ViewChildren() in Angular

@ViewChildren() is used to access multiple child components or DOM elements. It returns a QueryList.

Content projection

@ContentChild()

@ContentChild() is used to get a reference to content the parent projects into a component using <ng-content>.

  • The parent puts something inside the child component.
  • The child displays it with <ng-content>.
  • The child uses @ContentChild to access and control that content.
// Parent (usage)
<app-card>
  <button #actionBtn>Click Me</button>
</app-card>
// Child (Card)
<div class="card">
  <ng-content></ng-content>
</div>
import { Component, ContentChild, ElementRef, AfterContentInit } from '@angular/core';

@Component({
  selector: 'app-card',
  templateUrl: './card.component.html'
})
export class CardComponent implements AfterContentInit {
  @ContentChild('actionBtn') button!: ElementRef;

  ngAfterContentInit() {
    this.button.nativeElement.style.background = 'blue';
  }
}
Common use: reusable UI components with content projection, like cards, modals, or custom wrappers.
Dynamic views

TemplateRef

TemplateRef is a reference to an Angular template (<ng-template>). It acts as a blueprint, and is not rendered until Angular creates a view from it.

  • Used for dynamic view creation.
  • Used internally by *ngIf and *ngFor.
Dynamic views

ViewContainerRef

ViewContainerRef is a place in the DOM where Angular can add or remove views dynamically. It works with TemplateRef to render templates at runtime, and is used internally by features like *ngIf and *ngFor.

  • Used to load UI at runtime, and show or hide content dynamically.
Runtime rendering

Dynamic Component Loading

Dynamic component loading means creating and rendering a component at runtime, instead of defining it directly in the template — based on conditions or user actions.

  • Use cases: dynamic forms, modals / popups, role-based UI, dashboards.
@ViewChild('container', { read: ViewContainerRef })
container!: ViewContainerRef;

loadComponent() {
  this.container.clear();
  this.container.createComponent(LoginComponent);
}
<ng-template #container></ng-template>

ViewContainerRef.createComponent() is the method behind this. In real projects, it's a common way to build a reusable modal system or dynamic dashboard widgets — instead of hardcoding components in the HTML, different components load dynamically based on user action or an API response.

Template

ng-container

<ng-container> is used to group elements without creating an extra HTML element in the DOM. It's useful when you want a structural directive like *ngIf or *ngFor without adding an unnecessary <div>.

<ng-container *ngIf="isLoggedIn">
  <h1>Welcome</h1>
</ng-container>

No extra <div> is created in the rendered DOM.

Part B · Lifecycle & change detection
Lifecycle & change detection

How a component is born, updated, and torn down.

06

Lifecycle hooks

Overview

Angular Lifecycle Hooks

Lifecycle hooks are special methods that Angular calls automatically at different stages of a component or directive's life, from creation to destruction.

  1. constructor() — the class is created, dependencies are injected.
  2. ngOnChanges()
  3. ngOnInit()
  4. ngDoCheck()
  5. ngAfterContentInit()
  6. ngAfterContentChecked()
  7. ngAfterViewInit()
  8. ngAfterViewChecked()
  9. ngOnDestroy()
Lifecycle hook

ngOnChanges()

ngOnChanges() runs whenever an @Input() property's value changes. It works only with @Input() properties, and runs before ngOnInit() on the very first change.

Lifecycle hook

ngDoCheck()

ngDoCheck() runs on every Angular change detection cycle — even when Angular doesn't detect a change automatically. It's used for custom, manual change detection, mainly to catch changes Angular can't track on its own.

Lifecycle hook

ngAfterContentInit()

ngAfterContentInit() runs once, after Angular projects external content into a component using <ng-content>. It's mainly used with @ContentChild to access that projected content.

Lifecycle hook

ngAfterContentChecked()
