this.form.controls.nme;              // compile error: typo caught

Without nonNullable: true, reset() sets the value to null, so the type becomes string | null. Use FormBuilder.nonNullable.group({...}) as a shortcut.

Forms

setValue vs. patchValue

this.form.setValue({ name: 'Ram', age: 30 });     // all fields required, or it throws
this.form.patchValue({ name: 'Ram' });            // only the fields you give
this.form.reset({ name: '', age: null });         // back to the initial state: pristine and untouched

Use patchValue when filling a form from an API response that may not contain every field.

Forms

Form states: touched, dirty & valid

StateMeaning
pristine / dirtythe user has not / has changed the value
untouched / touchedthe field has not / has been blurred
valid / invalidvalidation passes / fails
pendingan async validator is running
disabledexcluded from the value and from validation
 
submit() {
  if (this.form.invalid) {
    this.form.markAllAsTouched();     // show every error message
    return;
  }
  this.api.save(this.form.getRawValue()).subscribe();
}
@if (form.controls.name.touched && form.controls.name.hasError('required')) {
  <small class="error">Name is required</small>
}

form.value leaves out disabled controls; getRawValue() includes them.

Forms

valueChanges & statusChanges

this.form.controls.pan.valueChanges.pipe(
  debounceTime(300),
  distinctUntilChanged(),
  switchMap(pan => this.api.verifyPan(pan)),
  takeUntilDestroyed()
).subscribe(result => this.panStatus = result);

this.form.controls.type.valueChanges.subscribe(t => {
  const gst = this.form.controls.gst;
  t === 'company' ? gst.enable() : gst.disable();     // react to another field
});

Pass { emitEvent: false } to setValue or patchValue to avoid triggering valueChanges and creating a loop. See debounceTime.

Forms

updateOn: blur & submit

name = new FormControl('', { validators: [Validators.required], updateOn: 'blur' });

form = new FormGroup({ /* ... */ }, { updateOn: 'submit' });    // validate only when submitted

It is a good choice for expensive validators, such as ones that call an API. Values are change (default), blur, or submit.

Forms

ngModel vs. formControlName

<!-- template-driven -->
<form #f="ngForm" (ngSubmit)="save(f)">
  <input name="email" [(ngModel)]="email" required email #e="ngModel">
  @if (e.invalid && e.touched) { <small>Invalid email</small> }
</form>

<!-- reactive -->
<form [formGroup]="form" (ngSubmit)="save()">
  <input formControlName="email">
</form>

Reactive forms are easier to test, handle dynamic and complex forms, and are synchronous. Both need FormsModule or ReactiveFormsModule imported. Do not mix ngModel with formControlName on the same input.

Forms

FormArray: add and remove rows

form = this.fb.group({
  deductees: this.fb.array([this.newRow()])
});

get deductees() { return this.form.controls.deductees; }

newRow() {
  return this.fb.group({ name: ['', Validators.required], amount: [0, Validators.min(1)] });
}
add() { this.deductees.push(this.newRow()); }
remove(i: number) { this.deductees.removeAt(i); }
<div formArrayName="deductees">
  @for (row of deductees.controls; track row; let i = $index) {
    <div [formGroupName]="i">
      <input formControlName="name">
      <input type="number" formControlName="amount">
      <button type="button" (click)="remove(i)">Remove</button>
    </div>
  }
</div>
<button type="button" (click)="add()">Add row</button>
33

HTTP, auth & security in practice

Angular 15+

Functional interceptors

export const authInterceptor: HttpInterceptorFn = (req, next) => {
  const token = inject(AuthService).token();
  const authReq = token
    ? req.clone({ setHeaders: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` } })   // requests are immutable: clone
    : req;
  return next(authReq);
};

export const errorInterceptor: HttpInterceptorFn = (req, next) =>
  next(req).pipe(
    catchError((err: HttpErrorResponse) => {
      if (err.status === 401) inject(Router).navigate(['/login']);
      return throwError(() => err);
    })
  );

// app.config.ts
provideHttpClient(withInterceptors([authInterceptor, errorInterceptor]))

Interceptors run in the order listed for the request, and in reverse for the response. Class-based interceptors still work with withInterceptorsFromDi(). See Interceptor.

Authentication

JWT auth & refresh token flow

Flow: login returns an access token (short-lived, minutes) and a refresh token (long-lived). Each request sends the access token. On a 401, call the refresh endpoint, store the new token, and replay the failed request.

export const refreshInterceptor: HttpInterceptorFn = (req, next) => {
  const auth = inject(AuthService);
  return next(req).pipe(
    catchError(err => {
      if (err.status !== 401 || req.url.includes('/refresh')) return throwError(() => err);
      return auth.refresh().pipe(                       // POST /refresh
        switchMap(token => next(req.clone({ setHeaders: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` } })))
      );
    })
  );
};
  • With several requests failing together, share one refresh call (shareReplay or a BehaviorSubject flag), so you do not refresh five times.
  • Where to store tokens: localStorage is simple but any XSS script can read it. An HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite cookie cannot be read by JavaScript. See localStorage vs. cookies.
HTTP

HttpParams, headers & request options

const params = new HttpParams().set('page', 2).set('status', 'paid');   // HttpParams is immutable
const headers = new HttpHeaders({ 'X-Tenant': 'abc' });

this.http.get<Order[]>('/api/orders', { params, headers });             // /api/orders?page=2&status=paid

this.http.get<Order[]>('/api/orders', { observe: 'response' })
  .subscribe(res => res.headers.get('X-Total-Count'));                  // full HttpResponse

this.http.get('/api/report', { responseType: 'blob' });                 // download a file
this.http.post('/api/orders', body);                                    // an object body is sent as JSON

Each .set() returns a new object; forgetting to use the return value is a common bug.

HTTP

File upload with progress

upload(file: File) {
  const body = new FormData();
  body.append('file', file);                       // do not set Content-Type: the browser adds the boundary

  return this.http.post('/api/upload', body, { reportProgress: true, observe: 'events' }).pipe(
    map(event => {
      if (event.type === HttpEventType.UploadProgress)
        return Math.round(100 * event.loaded / (event.total ?? 1));
      if (event.type === HttpEventType.Response) return 100;
      return 0;
    })
  );
}
<input type="file" accept=".xlsx,.csv" (change)="onFile($event)">
onFile(e: Event) {
  const file = (e.target as HTMLInputElement).files?.[0];
  if (file) this.upload(file).subscribe(p => this.progress = p);
}
HTTP

retry, timeout & firstValueFrom

this.http.get('/api/orders').pipe(
  timeout(10000),                                          // error after 10 seconds
  retry({ count: 3, delay: (err, n) => timer(n * 1000) }), // retry with a growing delay
  catchError(() => of([]))
);

const data = await firstValueFrom(this.http.get('/api/orders'));   // replaces toPromise()

Retry only safe calls such as GET; retrying a POST can create duplicates. toPromise() is deprecated; use firstValueFrom (first value) or lastValueFrom (last value before completion).

Security

XSRF, CSP & other security

  • XSS — Angular escapes bound values; see XSS & DomSanitizer. Avoid innerHTML with user data and bypassSecurityTrust*.
  • CSRF/XSRF — for cookie-based auth, the server sets a cookie named XSRF-TOKEN, and Angular copies it into the X-XSRF-TOKEN header on mutating requests (withXsrfConfiguration() changes the names). Bearer-token APIs are not affected.
  • CSP — a Content-Security-Policy header limits where scripts and styles can load from; Angular supports nonces with ngCspNonce.
  • Other: HTTPS only, HttpOnly cookies, validate on the server, never put secrets in the frontend bundle, keep dependencies updated (npm audit), and use route guards only for UX, since real authorization must be on the server.
HTTP

CORS & proxy.conf.json

{
  "/api": {
    "target": "http://localhost:3000",
    "secure": false,
    "changeOrigin": true,
    "pathRewrite": { "^/api": "" }
  }
}
"serve": { "options": { "proxyConfig": "proxy.conf.json" } }

CORS is a browser rule, fixed by the server's headers (see CORS). The proxy only works with ng serve; in production use a reverse proxy or CORS headers.

34

Build, tooling & source maps

Build

Source maps

A source map (.map file) links the minified, bundled JavaScript back to the original TypeScript, so DevTools and error trackers show your real file names and line numbers instead of main.js:1:48213.

"configurations": {
  "development": { "sourceMap": true, "optimization": false },
  "production":  { "sourceMap": false, "optimization": true }
}
  • Enabled in development by default and turned off in production, because they make the download bigger and expose your source code.
  • Options: "sourceMap": { "scripts": true, "styles": true, "hidden": true }. Hidden source maps are generated but not referenced from the bundle, so you can upload them privately to Sentry, or similar tools, to read production errors.
  • Browsers download the map only when DevTools is open, so it does not slow normal users.
  • Used with source-map-explorer to see what makes the bundle big. See Checking performance.
  • In tsconfig.json, "sourceMap": true makes tsc emit them; see tsconfig strict options.
Build

Angular build system: esbuild vs. webpack

  • **@angular-devkit/build-angular:browser — the older webpack** builder.
  • **@angular-devkit/build-angular:application — the newer builder based on esbuild (and Vite** for ng serve), the default since Angular 17. It builds much faster, and supports SSR and prerendering in one builder.
  • Webpack bundles by following imports; esbuild does the same, written in Go, so it is much faster.
  • To migrate: ng update @angular/cli --name use-application-builder.
  • Output has hashed file names (main-A1B2C3.js) for cache busting, and is split into lazy chunks per lazy route.
Build

Bundle budgets

"budgets": [
  { "type": "initial", "maximumWarning": "500kB", "maximumError": "1MB" },
  { "type": "anyComponentStyle", "maximumWarning": "2kB", "maximumError": "4kB" }
]

When a build exceeds the error limit it fails, which stops a heavy library from slipping into production. Fix it with lazy loading, removing unused imports, importing single functions instead of whole libraries, or analyzing with source-map-explorer or ng build --stats-json plus webpack-bundle-analyzer.

Build

What ng build --configuration production does

  • AOT compilation of templates.
  • Minification and dead-code removal (tree shaking); see Tree Shaking.
  • Bundling and code splitting for lazy routes.
  • Output hashing for cache busting.
  • Optimized fonts and CSS (inlined critical CSS).
  • Source maps off and environment file replacement.
  • Output goes to dist/<project>/browser and is deployed to any static host (Nginx, S3, Netlify, IIS).

For a single-page app the server must serve index.html for unknown paths (try_files $uri /index.html; in Nginx).

Build

Deploying an Angular app

server {
  listen 80;
  root /usr/share/nginx/html;
  location / {
    try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html;       # deep links like /returns/42 work after refresh
  }
  location ~* \.(js|css|woff2)$ {
    add_header Cache-Control "public, max-age=31536000, immutable";   # hashed files can be cached for a year
  }
}
FROM node:20 AS build
WORKDIR /app
COPY . .
RUN npm ci && npm run build

FROM nginx:alpine
COPY --from=build /app/dist/my-app/browser /usr/share/nginx/html
COPY nginx.conf /etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf
  • Do not cache index.html, so users get new deployments at once.
  • For a sub-folder deployment: ng build --base-href /erp/.
  • A 404 on refresh means the server is missing the rewrite rule.
Build

Runtime config & multiple environments

environment.ts values are replaced during the build, so one build per environment. To use one build everywhere, load a JSON file when the app starts:

// app.config.ts
provideAppInitializer(() => {
  const http = inject(HttpClient);
  const config = inject(ConfigService);
  return firstValueFrom(http.get<AppConfig>('assets/config.json')).then(c => config.set(c));
})

The older form is the APP_INITIALIZER token with multi: true. Angular waits for the Promise or Observable before the app starts, so the config is ready for every component. See environment.ts files.

Tooling

ESLint, Prettier & code quality

  • ESLint (ng add @angular-eslint/schematics, then ng lint) finds mistakes and bad patterns. TSLint is retired.
  • Prettier formats code, so reviews are about logic and not spacing.
  • Husky and lint-staged run both on git commit.
  • Commit conventions: Conventional Commits (feat:, fix:).
  • CI: run lint, test --watch=false, and build on each pull request.
  • Enable strict mode in tsconfig.json and strictTemplates in Angular; see tsconfig strict options.
Tooling

Upgrading Angular versions

ng update @angular/core@18 @angular/cli@18
ng update @angular/material@18
  • Upgrade one major version at a time; commit a clean git state first.
  • Use update.angular.dev for the checklist between two versions.
  • Migrations run automatically (for example to standalone, the new control flow, and the application builder).
  • Check that third-party libraries support the new version.
  • Angular releases a major version about every 6 months, and gives long-term support to each.
Tooling

Schematics & ng generate

ng generate component orders/order-list --standalone --change-detection OnPush
ng generate service core/auth
ng generate guard core/auth --functional
ng generate pipe shared/rupees
ng generate interface models/order
ng add @angular/material

Schematics are scripts that create or modify project files; ng add runs the schematic of a package to install and configure it. Teams write their own to generate feature folders in the project's standard layout. Set defaults in angular.json under schematics.

UI libraries

Angular Material & CDK

  • Angular Material — buttons, forms, tables, dialogs, snackbars, date pickers, and theming.
  • Angular CDK — unstyled building blocks for your own UI: Overlay, DragDrop, Clipboard, A11y (focus trap, live announcer), Portal, Table, and Virtual Scrolling.
  • Alternatives: PrimeNG, NG-ZORRO, Tailwind with Headless UI, Bootstrap with ng-bootstrap.

Material dialog example:

const ref = this.dialog.open(ConfirmComponent, { data: { text: 'Delete this challan?' }, width: '400px' });
ref.afterClosed().subscribe(ok => ok && this.delete());
Performance

Virtual scrolling

<cdk-virtual-scroll-viewport itemSize="48" class="viewport">
  <div *cdkVirtualFor="let row of rows" class="row">{{ row.name }}</div>
</cdk-virtual-scroll-viewport>
.viewport { height: 400px; }

Only the visible items, plus a small buffer, exist in the DOM. Other large-list tools: server-side pagination, trackBy, OnPush, and @defer. Compare with trackBy and Optimizing performance.

Features

i18n & PWA

  • i18n: mark text with the i18n attribute, run ng extract-i18n, translate the XLF file, and build one app per language. Libraries like ngx-translate and Transloco switch language at runtime instead.
  • PWA: ng add @angular/pwa adds a web manifest and the service worker (ngsw-config.json) that caches the app shell for offline use and enables install-to-home-screen. SwUpdate tells you when a new version is available.
<h1 i18n="@@title">Welcome</h1>

Dates, numbers and currencies use the LOCALE_ID; for Indian formats register en-IN and use provide: LOCALE_ID, useValue: 'en-IN'.

35

Architecture, state & testing

Architecture

Core, shared & feature structure

src/app/
  core/        singleton services, interceptors, guards, layout (loaded once)
  shared/      reusable components, pipes, directives (imported wherever needed)
  features/
    orders/    its own routes, pages, components, services (lazy loaded)
    returns/
  app.config.ts
  app.routes.ts
  • Features should not import each other; share through shared or core.
  • Keep smart components (pages) in the feature, and dumb components in shared if reused. See Smart vs. Dumb components.
  • With standalone components, a "module" is just a folder plus a routes file.
  • forRoot() / forChild() (a ModuleWithProviders) let a module provide singletons once at root and routes or config in children; standalone code uses provideX() functions instead.
Architecture

Facade & service-layer pattern

@Injectable({ providedIn: 'root' })
export class OrdersFacade {
  private api = inject(OrdersApi);
  private state = signal<Order[]>([]);
  readonly orders = this.state.asReadonly();
  readonly total = computed(() => this.state().reduce((s, o) => s + o.amount, 0));

  load() { this.api.getAll().subscribe(o => this.state.set(o)); }
  add(o: Order) { this.api.create(o).subscribe(saved => this.state.update(list => [...list, saved])); }
}

Components talk only to the facade, and never to HTTP or the store directly. This makes the data source easy to replace and the components easy to test.

State management

NgRx: actions, reducers, effects & selectors

export const loadOrders = createAction('[Orders] Load');
export const loadOrdersSuccess = createAction('[Orders] Load Success', props<{ orders: Order[] }>());

export const reducer = createReducer(
  { orders: [], loading: false },
  on(loadOrders, s => ({ ...s, loading: true })),
  on(loadOrdersSuccess, (s, { orders }) => ({ ...s, orders, loading: false }))
);

loadOrders$ = createEffect(() => this.actions$.pipe(
  ofType(loadOrders),
  switchMap(() => this.api.getAll().pipe(
    map(orders => loadOrdersSuccess({ orders })),
    catchError(() => of(loadOrdersFailure()))
  ))
));

export const selectOrders = createSelector(selectOrdersState, s => s.orders);
  • Store — the single state object. Action — what happened. Reducer — a pure function that returns new state. Effect — async side effects (API calls). Selector — a memoized read of part of the state.
  • Use it for large apps with a lot of shared state; for small apps a service with signals is enough. NgRx also has a lighter SignalStore and ComponentStore. See State Management and NgRx.
Architecture

Micro frontends & monorepos

  • Micro frontends: separate teams build and deploy parts of the UI independently. In Angular, this is usually done with Module Federation (@angular-architects/native-federation), where a shell app loads remote apps at runtime.
  • Monorepo: one repository with many apps and shared libraries, managed by Nx or the Angular CLI workspace (ng generate library). Benefits are shared code, one version of dependencies, and affected-only builds and tests.
  • Trade-offs: more complexity and shared-dependency version issues, so only use micro frontends when team size and deployment independence need them.
Error handling

Global ErrorHandler

@Injectable()
export class GlobalErrorHandler implements ErrorHandler {
  private logger = inject(LoggerService);
  handleError(error: unknown) {
    this.logger.send(error);              // send to Sentry or your backend
    console.error(error);
  }
}

// app.config.ts
{ provide: ErrorHandler, useClass: GlobalErrorHandler }

It catches errors in templates, lifecycle hooks, and event handlers; HTTP errors are best handled in an interceptor, and unhandled promise rejections also reach it through zone.js. See Handling API errors.

Services

Title & Meta services

constructor(private title: Title, private meta: Meta) {}

ngOnInit() {
  this.title.setTitle('Return 24Q - TDS Portal');
  this.meta.updateTag({ name: 'description', content: 'Quarterly TDS return summary' });
}

For route-level titles, use the title property of a route. Works with SSR, so crawlers see the tags.

Features

Angular Elements

const el = createCustomElement(WidgetComponent, { injector: this.injector });
customElements.define('tds-widget', el);
<tds-widget user-id="42"></tds-widget>

Used to embed an Angular widget in a non-Angular site (a CMS or a legacy page), or to share components between frameworks. See Web Components.

Testing

Testing async code: fakeAsync & waitForAsync

it('debounces the search', fakeAsync(() => {
  component.search.setValue('tds');
  tick(300);                                   // move the fake clock forward 300 ms
  expect(api.search).toHaveBeenCalledWith('tds');
}));

it('loads data', waitForAsync(() => {
  fixture.detectChanges();
  fixture.whenStable().then(() => {
    expect(component.orders.length).toBe(2);
  });
}));
  • fakeAsync + tick() / flush() for timers, debounce, and promises.
  • waitForAsync for real async work.
  • Newer test runners (Jest, Vitest, Web Test Runner) are common alternatives to Karma.
  • E2E tests: Cypress or Playwright. See TestBed.
Testing

Karma, Jasmine, Jest & coverage

Jasmine + KarmaJest
Runs ina real browserNode with jsdom
Speedslowerfaster
Mocksjasmine.createSpyjest.fn()
Default in Angularyes, for yearsoptional
 
ng test --code-coverage --watch=false

Coverage shows which lines your tests run; a common target is 70–80%. Test behavior, not private methods, and mock HTTP with HttpTestingController. See Mocking with spies.

Debugging

Debugging an Angular app

  • Open DevTools, go to Sources, find your .ts file thanks to source maps, and set breakpoints.
  • Use debugger; for a breakpoint from code.
  • Angular DevTools shows the component tree, inputs, and change detection profile.
  • In development, ng.getComponent($0) in the console returns the component of the selected element.
  • Use tap(console.log) inside an RxJS pipe to see a stream's values; see tap.
  • The Network tab shows failed calls, and Performance shows slow rendering.
  • VS Code can attach the Chrome debugger with a launch.json configuration.
